\displaystyle\lim_{\gamma\to 0}

\displaystyle\Big[\gamma^{2}\log\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\Big(\frac{2\xi}{\gamma}\Big)-\gamma^{2}\log\frac{\gamma}{2}\int^{\mathrm{Re}(\xi)}_{0}(\xi-x-\frac{1}{2})dx-\gamma^{2}\log\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\Big(\frac{2\mathbf{i}\mathrm{Im}(\xi)}{\gamma}\Big)\Big]

(6.2)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-4\mathrm{Re}(\xi)\log\sqrt{2\pi}+\int^{\mathrm{Re}(\xi)}_{0}\log\Gamma(\xi-x)dx&\text{if }\mathrm{Re}(\xi)>0,\\
4\mathrm{Re}(\xi)\log\sqrt{2\pi}+\int^{0}_{\mathrm{Re}(\xi)}\log\Gamma(\xi+x)dx&\text{if }\mathrm{Re}(\xi)<0.\end{cases}

Here
\log\Gamma(\cdot)
is taken w.r.t. the principle branch of logarithm.

Proof.

We prove the above statement for
\mathrm{Re}(\xi)>0
. The remaining case follows from a similar argument. From the identities of the double Gamma function, we obtain the following shift relation for the function
\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}(.)
for any
\mathrm{Re}(\xi)>0

[
57
]
:

(6.3)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(z+\chi\right)=\sqrt{2\pi}\frac{\chi^{\chi z-\frac{1}{2}}}{\Gamma(\chi z)}\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}(z),

\displaystyle\chi=\frac{2}{\gamma},\frac{\gamma}{2},

which can also be expressed as

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\Big(\frac{2\xi}{\gamma}-n\frac{\gamma}{2}\Big)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big(\frac{\gamma}{2}\Big)^{-\xi+(n+1)\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}+\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma\big(\xi-(n+1)\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\big)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\Big(\frac{2\xi}{\gamma}-(n+1)\frac{\gamma}{2}\Big).

Applying the above relation for all
n
such that
n\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\leq\mathrm{Re}(\xi)
, we get

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\Big(\frac{2\mathbf{i}\mathrm{Im}\xi}{\gamma}+\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\{\frac{4\mathrm{Re}(\xi)}{\gamma^{2}}\right\}\Big)=\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\Big(\frac{2\xi}{\gamma}\Big)\prod_{n=0}^{\lfloor\frac{4\mathrm{Re}(\xi)}{\gamma^{2}}-1\rfloor}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big(\frac{\gamma}{2}\Big)^{-\xi+(n+1)\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}+\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma\big(\xi-(n+1)\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\big).

where
\left\{\frac{4\mathrm{Re}(\xi)}{\gamma^{2}}\right\}
denotes the fractional part of
\frac{4\mathrm{Re}(\xi)}{\gamma^{2}}
. Taking logarithms on both sides of the above equation, multiplying by
\gamma^{2}
and taking the limit
\gamma\to 0
, we arrive at (
6.2
). The expression for
\mathrm{Re}(\xi)<0
is obtained similarly.
∎

In the next result, we utilize this asymptotics to find semi-classical limit of
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma,P}(\alpha)
.

Proposition 6.1
.

Consider the scaling
\alpha=\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\gamma}
,
P=\frac{P_{0}}{\gamma}
with
\alpha_{0}\in(-4,2)
,
P_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
. As
\gamma\to 0
, the semi-classical limit of
\mathcal{A}_{\gamma,P}(\alpha)
has the following expression:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}(P_{0},\alpha_{0})

\displaystyle:=\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\gamma^{2}\log\mathcal{A}_{\gamma,P}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{i}\pi\alpha_{0}\left(\alpha_{0}+\mathbf{i}P_{0}\right)}{2}+4\alpha_{0}\sqrt{2\pi}-\int_{\mathrm{Re}\left(1-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)}^{\mathrm{Re}(1)+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}}\log\Gamma\left(1-x-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)dx

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathrm{Re}\left(1+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)}^{\mathrm{Re}(1+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2})+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}}\log\Gamma\left(1-x+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)dx-\int_{\mathrm{Re}\left(1-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)}^{\mathrm{Re}(1-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2})+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}}\log\Gamma\left(1-x-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)dx

(6.4)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathrm{Re}\left(1+\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)}^{\mathrm{Re}(1+\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2})+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}}\log\Gamma\left(1-x+\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)dx.

Proof.

With the scaling
\alpha=\alpha_{0}/\gamma
,
P=P_{0}/\gamma
, and using the shift relations of the double gamma function as noted in (
6.3
) with
\chi=\frac{\gamma}{2}
, the expression (
6.1
) simplifies as

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\gamma,P_{0}}(\alpha_{0})

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{\mathbf{i}\pi\alpha_{0}\left(\alpha_{0}+\mathbf{i}P_{0}\right)}{2\gamma^{2}}}\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\right)^{\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\gamma^{2}}}\frac{\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{\gamma}-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}+\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{\gamma}-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2\gamma}\right)}{\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}+\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{\gamma}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\times\frac{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(1+\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}+\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}\right)}.

Taking logarithm of both sides of the above equation, multiplying both sides of the equation with
\gamma
and letting
\gamma\to 0
reads

(6.5)

\displaystyle\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\gamma^{2}\lim\mathcal{A}_{\gamma,P_{0}}(\alpha_{0})

(6.6)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{i}\pi\alpha_{0}\left(\alpha_{0}+\mathbf{i}P_{0}\right)}{2}+\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\gamma^{2}\log\left(\frac{\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{\gamma}-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}+\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{\gamma}-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2\gamma}\right)}{\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}+\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{\gamma}\right)}\right).

Observe that the last term in the expression above can be written as a product of ratios of the form
\frac{\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}(\xi-\alpha_{0}/4)\right)}{\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2\xi}{\gamma}\right)}
. It is therefore sufficient to study the term

\displaystyle\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\gamma^{2}\log\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}(\xi-\alpha_{0}/4)\right)-\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\gamma^{2}\log\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2\xi}{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle\mathop{=}^{\eqref{eq:DoubleGammaAsymptote}}\alpha_{0}\sqrt{2\pi}+\int_{-\infty}^{\mathrm{Re}\left(\xi-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)}\log\Gamma\left(\xi-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}-x\right)dx-\int_{-\infty}^{\mathrm{Re}\left(\xi\right)}\log\Gamma\left(\xi-x\right)dx

\displaystyle=\alpha_{0}\sqrt{2\pi}-\int_{\mathrm{Re}\left(\xi-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)}^{\mathrm{Re}(\xi)+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}}\log\Gamma\left(\xi-x-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)dx.

Simplifying the expression (
6.6
) using the identity above proves the statement of this proposition.

∎

Theorem 6.1
.

For any
|q|<r
for some very small
r>0
, partition function
Z^{\alpha}_{\gamma,P}(q)
has the following asymptotics as
\gamma
approaches
0
,

(6.7)

\displaystyle\lim_{\gamma\rightarrow 0}\gamma^{2}\log Z^{\alpha}_{\gamma,P}(q)=\frac{\alpha_{0}^{2}}{8}\log q+\alpha_{0}^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\log(1-q^{2k})+\mathcal{B}(P_{0},\alpha_{0}),

or equivalently

(6.8)

\displaystyle\lim_{\gamma\rightarrow 0}\gamma^{2}\log Z^{\alpha}_{\gamma,P}(q)=\frac{\alpha_{0}^{2}}{24}\log q+\alpha_{0}^{2}\log\eta(q)+\mathcal{B}(P_{0},\alpha_{0}).

where
\alpha_{0}\in(-4,2)
,
P_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, with
\mathcal{B}(P_{0},\alpha_{0})
defined in Proposition
6.1
.

Proof.

Recall from (
2.15
) that

Z^{\alpha}_{\gamma,P}=q^{\frac{1}{12}(\frac{\alpha\gamma}{2}+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}-1)}\eta(q)^{\alpha^{2}+1-\frac{\alpha\gamma}{2}}\mathcal{A}_{\gamma,P}(\alpha).

Taking logarithm on both sides and multiplying by
\gamma^{2}
yields

\gamma^{2}\log Z^{\alpha}_{\gamma,P}=\gamma^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}_{0}}{24\gamma^{2}}+\frac{1}{12}\big(\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}-1\big)\right)\log q+\gamma^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}_{0}}{\gamma^{2}}+\big(\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}-1\big)\right)\log\eta(q)+\gamma^{2}\log\mathcal{A}_{\gamma,P}(\alpha)

where we have used the scaling rule
\alpha=\alpha_{0}/\gamma
. Finally, using the product representation of the eta function (
A.8
), and letting
\gamma\to 0
both sides yields the desired conclusion.

∎

Theorem 6.2
.

For
q\in(0,1)
, as
q\to 0
, the semi-classical deformed conformal block
\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\gamma^{2}\log\psi^{\alpha}_{\gamma/2}
in (
4.154
) behaves as

(6.9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{\gamma\rightarrow 0}\gamma^{2}\log\left(\theta_{1}(z)^{\frac{\chi}{4}\left(\chi-\alpha\right)}\psi^{\alpha_{0}/\gamma}_{\gamma/2}(z,q)\right)\\
&=-\alpha_{0}\left(\alpha_{0}+4\right)\log(2\pi)+\left(\frac{P_{0}^{2}}{2}-\frac{\alpha_{0}(\alpha_{0}+2)}{12}\right)\log q+\mathcal{B}(P_{0},\alpha_{0}),\end{split}

where
\alpha_{0}\in(-4,2)
,
P_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\mathcal{B}(P_{0},\alpha_{0})
is given by (
6.4
).

Proof.

From (
4.167
) and (
4.166
), we observe that, near
\gamma\to 0
, the deformed conformal block behaves as

(6.10)

\displaystyle\psi^{\alpha_{0}/\gamma}_{\gamma/2}(z,q)=q^{\frac{P_{0}^{2}}{2\gamma^{2}}-\frac{\alpha_{0}^{2}}{24\gamma^{2}}}\theta_{1}^{\prime}(0)^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}(4+\alpha_{0})}{6\gamma^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int^{1}_{0}\theta_{1}(x)^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}}e^{\pi P_{0}x}e^{\frac{\gamma}{2}Y(x;q)}dx\right)^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\gamma^{2}}}\right].

Now, using the expressions for theta functions (
A.3
) and (
A.6
), we can obtain the following behaviour near
q\to 0

\displaystyle\lim_{\gamma\rightarrow 0}\gamma^{2}\log\left(\theta_{1}(z)^{\frac{\gamma}{4}\left(\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\gamma}\right)}\psi^{\alpha_{0}/\gamma}_{\gamma/2}(z,q)\right)

\displaystyle=-\alpha_{0}\left(\alpha_{0}+4\right)\log(2\pi)+\left(\frac{P_{0}^{2}}{2}-\frac{\alpha_{0}(\alpha_{0}+2)}{12}\right)\log q+

(6.11)

\displaystyle+\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\gamma^{2}\log\mathbb{E}\left[\left(q^{\frac{\alpha_{0}}{8}}\int^{1}_{0}\theta_{1}(x)^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}}e^{\pi P_{0}x}e^{\frac{\gamma}{2}Y(x;q)}dx\right)^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\gamma^{2}}}\right].

As a final step, we note that the
\gamma\to 0
and
q\to 0
limit of the following

\mathcal{T}:=\gamma^{2}\log\mathbb{E}\left[\left(q^{\frac{\alpha_{0}}{8}}\int^{1}_{0}\theta_{1}(x)^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}}e^{\pi P_{0}x}e^{\frac{\gamma}{2}Y(x;q)}dx\right)^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\gamma^{2}}}\right]

commute. To see this, using Girsanov’s theorem, we write the above expression as (see
[
24
, Eq. 2.225]
)

(6.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}=(\alpha^{2}_{0}-\alpha_{0}\gamma/2)\log\left(q^{-1/12}\eta(q)\right)+\gamma^{2}\log\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2\gamma}F(0;q)}\mathcal{Q}(q)\left(\int_{0}^{1}(2\sin\pi x)^{-\alpha_{0}/2}e^{\pi P_{0}x}e^{\frac{\gamma}{2}Y(x)}dx\right)^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\gamma^{2}}}\right],

where
F(0;q)
and
\mathcal{Q}(q)
are defined as in (
4.49
). We can then decompose the above expression into
q
-dependent and
q
-independent terms. One can then repeat the arguments in the proof of Proposition
4.2
to prove that
\lim_{q\to 0}\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\mathcal{T}=\lim_{q\to 0}\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\mathcal{T}
. Consequently, we get

\lim_{q\to 0}\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\mathcal{T}=\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\gamma^{2}\log\mathcal{A}_{\gamma,P}(\alpha)=\mathcal{B}(P_{0},\alpha_{0}).

Taking
q\to 0
on both sides of (
6.11
) and substituting
\mathcal{B}(P_{0},\alpha_{0})
in place of
\lim_{q\to 0}\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\mathcal{T}
gives us (
6.9
).

∎

Theorem 6.3
.

Consider
z={\sf a}\tau+{\sf b}
for some
{\sf a},{\sf b}\in(0,1)
,
{\sf a}\neq\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}
. As
\tau\to\mathbf{i}\infty
, the semi-classical deformed conformal block
\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\gamma^{2}\log\psi^{\alpha_{0}/\gamma}_{2/\gamma}(z,q)
in (
4.1
) has the following behaviour with
\alpha_{0}\in(-2,2)
,
P_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
:

(6.13)

\displaystyle\lim_{\gamma\rightarrow 0}\gamma^{2}\log\left(\theta_{1}(z)^{\frac{\chi}{2}\left(\chi-\alpha\right)}\psi^{\alpha_{0}/\gamma}_{2/\gamma}(z,q)\right)

(6.14)

\displaystyle=C(\tau)+2\pi P_{0}(z+1)+\int_{\mathrm{Re}\left(\frac{2-3\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)}^{\mathrm{Re}(-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2})+\frac{\alpha_{0}-2}{4}}\log\Gamma\left(-x+\frac{2-3\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathrm{Re}\left(\frac{2+\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)}^{\mathrm{Re}(\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2})+\frac{\alpha_{0}-2}{4}}\log\Gamma\left(-x+\frac{2+\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)dx-\int_{\mathrm{Re}\left(\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}+\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{2}\right)}^{\mathrm{Re}(\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}+\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{4})+\frac{\alpha_{0}-2}{4}}\log\Gamma\left(\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}-x+\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{2}\right)dx

(6.15)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathrm{Re}\left(-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}\right)}^{\mathrm{Re}(-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}-\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{4})+\frac{\alpha_{0}-2}{4}}\log\Gamma\left(-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}-x\right)dx,

where

(6.16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(\tau):=\frac{(2-\alpha_{0})(4+\alpha_{0})}{6}\log(2\pi)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{i}\pi(2-\alpha_{0})+\left(\frac{(\alpha_{0}-2)(\alpha_{0}-8)}{24}\right)\log q-2\pi\mathbf{i}(2-\alpha_{0})({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b}).

Proof.

Recalling the expressions (
4.20
) and (
4.22
), we obtain the following behaviour for the deformed conformal block in the case
\chi=\frac{2}{\gamma}
:

(6.17)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{\gamma\rightarrow 0}\gamma^{2}\log\left(\theta_{1}(z)^{\frac{\chi}{2}\left(\chi-\alpha\right)}\psi^{\alpha_{0}/\gamma}_{2/\gamma}(z,q)\right)\\
&=\frac{(2-\alpha_{0})(4+\alpha_{0})}{6}\log(2\pi)+\left(\frac{(\alpha_{0}-2)(\alpha_{0}-8)}{24}\right)\log q+2P_{0}z\pi\\
&+\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\gamma^{2}\log\mathbb{E}\left[\left(q^{\frac{\alpha_{0}-2}{8}}\int_{0}^{1}\theta_{1}(z+x)\theta_{1}(x)^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}}e^{\pi P_{0}x}e^{\frac{\gamma}{2}Y_{\tau}(x)}dx\right)^{\frac{(2-\alpha_{0})}{\gamma^{2}}}\right].\end{split}

We identify
z
with the solution of the non-autonomous elliptic Calogero-Moser model
u(\tau)
by letting
z=2u(\tau)
. As
\tau\to\mathbf{i}\infty
, the variable
u(\tau)={\sf a}\tau+{\sf b}
, with
{\sf a},{\sf b}
parameterization the A, B-cycle monodromies of the linear system associated to the non-autonomous elliptic Calogero-Moser model (see Appendix B). From (
A.3
), we then obtain the following behaviour of the theta function in the same limit:

\displaystyle\theta_{1}(z)\equiv\theta_{1}(2u(\tau))

\displaystyle=-2q^{1/4}\sin(2\pi u(\tau))\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}(1-q^{2k})(1-2\cos(4\pi u(\tau))q^{2k}+q^{4k})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{i}q^{1/4}e^{-2\mathbf{i}\pi({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b})}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(q^{2})\right),

and resultantly, as
\tau\to\mathbf{i}\infty
,

\displaystyle\theta_{1}(z+x)=\mathbf{i}q^{1/4}e^{-2\mathbf{i}\pi({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b})-\mathbf{i}\pi x}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(q^{2})\right).

Plugging the above expressions into the right hand side of (
6.17
) shows that (see also
[
24
, Lemma 5.3]
)

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to\mathbf{i}\infty}

\displaystyle\Big[\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\gamma^{2}\log\left(\theta_{1}(z)^{\frac{\chi}{2}\left(\chi-\alpha\right)}\psi^{\alpha_{0}/\gamma}_{2/\gamma}(z,q)\right)-\mathcal{C}(\tau)\Big]

(6.18)

\displaystyle=\lim_{q\to 0}\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\gamma^{2}\log\mathbb{E}\left[\left(q^{\frac{\alpha_{0}}{8}}\int_{0}^{1}\theta_{1}(x)^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}}e^{\pi x(P_{0}-\mathbf{i})}e^{\frac{\gamma}{2}Y(x;q)}dx\right)^{\frac{(2-\alpha_{0})}{\gamma^{2}}}\right],

where
C(\tau)
is defined in (
6.16
). Due to Proposition
4.2
, the limits
\lim_{q\to 0}
and
\lim_{\gamma\to 0}
commute. So, we first begin with the
q\to 0
limit

(6.19)

\displaystyle\lim_{q\to 0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(q^{\frac{\alpha_{0}}{8}}\int_{0}^{1}\theta_{1}(x)^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}}e^{\pi x(P_{0}-\mathbf{i})}e^{\frac{\gamma}{2}Y(x;q)}dx\right)^{\frac{(2-\alpha_{0})}{\gamma^{2}}}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{1}(\sin\pi x)^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}}e^{\pi x(P_{0}-\mathbf{i})}e^{\frac{\gamma}{2}{Y(x)}}dx\right)^{\frac{(2-\alpha_{0})}{\gamma^{2}}}\right].

We now note the following identity
[
3
]

(6.20)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{1}(\sin\pi x)^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}}e^{\pi x(P_{0}-\mathbf{i})}e^{\frac{\gamma}{2}{Y(x)}}dx\right)^{\frac{(2-\alpha_{0})}{\gamma^{2}}}\right]

(6.21)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi P_{0}/\gamma^{2}}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}}{4}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{\gamma}+\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{\gamma}\right)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\right)^{\frac{2}{\gamma}\left(\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{2\gamma}\right)}\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{0}}{\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{\gamma}+\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{2\gamma}\right)\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{\gamma}-\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{2\gamma}\right)}.

To simplify the above equation, we first note that the expression above can be written in terms of ratios of the form

\frac{\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}\left(\xi+\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)\right)}{\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}\xi\right)}.

We then use the identity

\displaystyle\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\gamma^{2}\log\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma}(\xi+\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{4})\right)-\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\gamma^{2}\log\Gamma_{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\left(\frac{2\xi}{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle\mathop{=}^{\eqref{eq:DoubleGammaAsymptote}}\left(\alpha_{0}-2\right)\sqrt{2\pi}+\int_{-\infty}^{\mathrm{Re}\left(\xi+\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)}\log\Gamma\left(\xi+\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{4}-x\right)dx-\int_{-\infty}^{\mathrm{Re}\left(\xi\right)}\log\Gamma\left(\xi-x\right)dx

\displaystyle=\left(\alpha_{0}-2\right)\sqrt{2\pi}-\int_{\mathrm{Re}\left(\xi+\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)}^{\mathrm{Re}(\xi)+\frac{\alpha_{0}-2}{4}}\log\Gamma\left(\xi-x+\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)dx.

Then, we repeat the procedure used in the proof of Proposition
6.1
. One can obtain similar expressions to the one above for the remaining terms in (
6.21
). Substituting them in (
6.18
), we obtain the expression

\displaystyle\lim_{q\to 0}\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\gamma^{2}\log\mathbb{E}\left[\left(q^{\frac{\alpha_{0}}{8}}\int_{0}^{1}\theta_{1}(x)^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}}e^{\pi x(P_{0}-\mathbf{i})}e^{\frac{\gamma}{2}Y(x)}dx\right)^{\frac{(2-\alpha_{0})}{\gamma^{2}}}\right]

\displaystyle\mathop{=}^{\eqref{A:reexp},\eqref{eq:Asymptotics}}2\pi P_{0}+\int_{\mathrm{Re}\left(\frac{2-3\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)}^{\mathrm{Re}(-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2})+\frac{\alpha_{0}-2}{4}}\log\Gamma\left(-x+\frac{2-3\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathrm{Re}\left(\frac{2+\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)}^{\mathrm{Re}(\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2})+\frac{\alpha_{0}-2}{4}}\log\Gamma\left(-x+\frac{2+\alpha_{0}}{4}\right)dx-\int_{\mathrm{Re}\left(\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}+\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{2}\right)}^{\mathrm{Re}(\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}+\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{4})+\frac{\alpha_{0}-2}{4}}\log\Gamma\left(\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}-x+\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{2}\right)dx

(6.22)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathrm{Re}\left(-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}\right)}^{\mathrm{Re}(-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}-\frac{2-\alpha_{0}}{4})+\frac{\alpha_{0}-2}{4}}\log\Gamma\left(-\frac{\mathbf{i}P_{0}}{2}-x\right)dx,

completing the proof of this Theorem.

∎

Appendix A
Useful notation and elliptic functions

The parameter
\gamma\in(0,2)
defines the central charge of the CFT as

(A.1)

\displaystyle c=1+6Q^{2},

\displaystyle Q:=\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{2}{\gamma}.

We use two other parameters

(A.2)

\displaystyle l_{\chi}=\frac{\chi^{2}}{2}-\frac{\alpha\chi}{2},

\displaystyle\chi=\left\{\frac{2}{\gamma},\frac{\gamma}{2}\right\}.

The modular parameter
\tau\in\mathbb{H}
and the nome is defined as
q:=e^{\mathbf{i}\pi\tau}
. The elliptic theta functions
\theta_{i}(z)=\theta_{i}(z|\tau)
are defined as
[
50
, 20.5(i)]

(A.3)

\displaystyle\theta_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=2q^{1/4}\sin(\pi z)\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}(1-q^{2k})(1-2\cos(2\pi z)q^{2k}+q^{4k}),

(A.4)

\displaystyle\theta_{2}(z)

\displaystyle=2q^{1/4}\cos(\pi z)\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}(1-q^{2k})(1+2\cos(2\pi z)q^{2k}+q^{4k})

(A.5)

\displaystyle\theta_{3}(z)

\displaystyle=\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}(1-q^{2k})(1+2\cos(2\pi z)q^{2k-1}+q^{4k})

(A.6)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\theta_{1}(z)|_{z=0}=\theta_{1}^{\prime}(0)

\displaystyle=2\pi q^{1/4}\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}\left(1-q^{2k}\right)^{3}=2\pi\eta(q)^{3}.

They have the following periodicity properties

(A.7)

\displaystyle\theta_{1}(z+1)=-\theta_{1}(z),

\displaystyle\theta_{1}(z+\tau)=-q^{-1}e^{-2\pi iz}\theta_{1}(z).

The Dedekind
\eta
function is defined as

(A.8)

\displaystyle\eta(q)

\displaystyle:=q^{\frac{1}{12}}\prod_{k=1}^{\infty}(1-q^{2k}),

and its logarithmic derivative

(A.9)

\displaystyle\eta_{1}(\tau)=-2\pi i\partial_{\tau}\log\eta(\tau)=-\frac{1}{6}\frac{\theta_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}(0)}{\theta_{1}^{\prime}(0)}.

The above expression is a direct consequence of the fact that the theta function
\theta_{1}(z)
solves the heat equation
\partial_{\tau}\theta_{1}(z)=\frac{1}{4\pi\mathbf{i}}\theta_{1}^{\prime\prime}(z)
.

The Weierstrass cubic is

(A.10)

\displaystyle\left(\wp^{\prime}(z)\right)^{2}=4\wp^{3}(z)-g_{2}\wp(z)-g_{3}.

The Weierstrass
\wp
-function solves the cubic above and is given by
[
50
, 23.2(ii)]

(A.11)

\displaystyle\wp(z)=\frac{1}{z^{2}}+\sum_{w\in\mathbb{L}\backslash\{0\}}\left(\frac{1}{(z-w)^{2}}-\frac{1}{w^{2}}\right),

where
\mathbb{L}
denotes the torus (doubly periodic lattice). The above function has a double pole at
z=0
, and is doubly periodic

(A.12)

\displaystyle\wp(z+1)=\wp(z),

\displaystyle\wp(z+\tau)=\wp(z).

Appendix B
Non-autonomous elliptic Calogero-Moser model

B.1.
Hamiltonian and the action

The 2-particle nonautonomous elliptic Calogero-Moser system is represented by the Hamiltonian

(B.1)

\displaystyle H(\tau)=v(\tau)^{2}-m^{2}\wp(2u(\tau)|\tau),

and the equations of motion read

(B.2)

\displaystyle v(\tau)=2\pi\mathbf{i}\frac{du(\tau)}{d\tau},

\displaystyle(2\pi\mathbf{i})\frac{dv(\tau)}{d\tau}=m^{2}\wp^{\prime}(2u|\tau).

The above equations of motion give the elliptic form of the Painlevé VI equation for special values of the parameters (see
[
18
, Chapter 1]
,
[
17
,
62
]
).

The equation (
B.2
) is
integrable
in the sense that it has an associated
Lax pair

\displaystyle\partial_{z}Y(z,\tau):=L(z,\tau)Y(z,\tau)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}v(\tau)&mx(2u(\tau),z)\\
mx(-2u(\tau),z)&-v(\tau)\end{array}\right)Y(z,\tau),

\displaystyle 2\pi\mathbf{i}\partial_{\tau}Y(z,\tau):=M(z,\tau)Y(z,\tau)=m\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&y(2u(\tau),z)\\
y(-2u(\tau),z)&0\end{array}\right)Y(z,\tau),

and (
B.2
) is given by the condition

(B.7)

\displaystyle 2\pi\mathbf{i}\partial_{z}\partial_{\tau}Y(z,\tau)=2\pi\mathbf{i}\partial_{z}\partial_{\tau}Y(z,\tau)\Rightarrow\partial_{\tau}L(z,\tau)-\partial_{z}M(z,\tau)+[L(z,\tau),M(z,\tau)]=0.

In the above expressions, the functions
x(\xi,z)
,
y(\xi,z)
(
B.1
) are defined as

(B.8)

\displaystyle x(\xi,z):=\frac{\theta_{1}(z-\xi|\tau)\theta_{1}^{\prime}(0|\tau)}{\theta_{1}(z|\tau)\theta_{1}(\xi|\tau)},

\displaystyle y(\xi,z):=\partial_{\xi}x(\xi,z).

Furthermore, the Hamiltonian (
B.1
) is
given by the A-cycle contour integral

(B.9)

H(\tau)=\oint_{A}dz\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}L^{2}(z,\tau)=v(\tau)^{2}-m^{2}\wp\left(2u(\tau)\right).

The functions
(u(\tau),v(\tau))
are the canonical coordinates of the system and they describe the position and momentum of a particle in an elliptic potential respectively.
Therefore, the Lagrangian of the above system is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=v(\tau)^{2}+m^{2}\wp\left(2u(\tau)\right),

and the action functional is defined as the integral of the Lagrangian

(B.10)

\displaystyle S(\tau):=\int^{\tau}\left(v(\tau^{\prime})^{2}+m^{2}\wp(2u(\tau^{\prime}))\right)\frac{d\tau^{\prime}}{2\pi\mathbf{i}}.

It is a known fact from classical mechanics that the action solves the Hamilton-Jacobi equation

\displaystyle 2\pi\mathbf{i}\partial_{\tau}S=\left(\partial_{u(\tau)}S\right)^{2}-m^{2}\wp(2u(\tau))S.

B.2.
Monodromy data

The monodromy data of the linear system (
B.1
) is obtained by observing that
L,M
are defined on a torus with a simple pole at
z=0
and satisfy the relations
[
17
]

(B.11)

\displaystyle L(z+1,\tau)=L(z,\tau),

\displaystyle L(z+\tau,\tau)=e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}u(t)\sigma_{3}}L(z,\tau)e^{-2\pi\mathbf{i}u(\tau)\sigma_{3}}.

Subsequently, the solution
Y(z,\tau)
(
B.1
) has the following monodromy properties around the A, B cycles of the torus and around the puncture
z=0
:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}Y(z+1,\tau)=M_{A}Y(z,\tau),\qquad Y(z+\tau,\tau)=M_{B}Y(z,\tau)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}u(\tau)\sigma_{3}},\\
\\
Y(e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}}z,\tau)=M_{0}Y(z,\tau),\end{array}

with the constraint

(B.15)

M_{0}=M_{A}^{-1}M_{B}^{-1}M_{A}M_{B}.

Choosing the matrix
M_{A}
to be diagonal, one obtains the following explicit expressions for the monodromy matrices

\displaystyle M_{A}=e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}{\sf a}\sigma_{3}},

\displaystyle M_{0}\sim e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}m\sigma_{3}},

\displaystyle M_{B}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\sin\pi(2({\sf a}-m))}{\sin 2\pi{\sf a}}e^{-\mathbf{i}\nu/2}&\frac{\sin\pi m}{\sin 2\pi{\sf a}}e^{\mathbf{i}\nu/2}\\
\\
-\frac{\sin\pi m}{\sin 2\pi{\sf a}}e^{-\mathbf{i}\nu/2}&\frac{\sin\pi(2({\sf a}+m)}{\sin 2\pi{\sf a}}e^{\mathbf{i}\nu/2}\end{array}\right),

where
\sim
denotes the conjugacy class,
\sigma_{3}
is the Pauli sigma matrix, and
m
is the parameter of the equation (
B.2
).

B.3.
Asymptotic behaviour of
u(\tau)

For the sake of completeness, in this section we detail the asymptotics of the solution of the NAECM model
u(\tau)
for
\tau\to\mathbf{i}\infty
, and
\tau\to\tau_{\star}
that were also computed in
[
11
,
9
]
.

Proposition B.1
.

In the limit
\tau\to\mathbf{i}\infty
, the solution of the equation (
B.2
)
u(\tau)
behaves as

(B.19)

\displaystyle u(\tau)={\sf a}\tau+{\sf b}+\frac{m^{2}}{8\pi\mathbf{i}{\sf a}^{2}}e^{4\pi\mathbf{i}({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b})}+\mathcal{O}(qe^{4\pi\mathbf{i}({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b})}),

where
q=e^{\mathbf{i}\pi\tau}
,
{\sf a}
,
m
parameterize the monodromies around the A-cycle and the puncture respectively (
B.2
), and the monodromy around the B-cycle is related to
{\sf b}
as

(B.20)

\displaystyle e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}{\sf b}}:=e^{\mathbf{i}\nu/2}\frac{\Gamma(2{\sf a}-m)\Gamma(1-2{\sf a})}{\Gamma(2{\sf a})\Gamma(1-m-2{\sf a})}.

Proof.

Our starting point is the following identity satisfied by the function
u(\tau)
that was shown in
[
11
, equation (3.56)]
(see also
[
19
]
):

(B.21)

\displaystyle\frac{\theta_{3}(2u|2\tau)}{\theta_{2}(2u|2\tau)}=\frac{Z_{0}^{D}(\tau)}{Z_{1/2}^{D}(\tau)},

where
Z_{0,1/2}^{D}
are dual Nekrasov-Okounkov functions with integer and half integer shifts and are defined as

(B.22)

\displaystyle Z_{\epsilon/2}^{D}:=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}C_{n}e^{\mathbf{i}n\nu}q^{2({\sf a}+n)^{2}}\mathcal{B}({\sf a}+n,m,q).

The coefficients

(B.23)

\displaystyle C_{n}=\frac{G(1-m+2({\sf a}+n))G(1-m-2({\sf a}+n))}{G(1+2({\sf a}+n))G(1-2({\sf a}+n))}\times\frac{G(1+2{\sf a})G(1-2{\sf a})}{G(1-m+2{\sf a})G(1-m-2{\sf a})},

and the combinatorial term
\mathcal{B}(.)
is given by

(B.24)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}(a,m,q)=\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\left(1-q^{2n}\right)^{1-2m^{2}}\sum_{Y_{+},Y_{-}}q^{2\left(|Y_{+}|+|Y_{-}|\right)}\prod_{\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime}=\pm}\frac{N_{Y_{\epsilon},Y_{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(m+(\epsilon-\epsilon^{\prime}){\sf a})}{N_{Y_{\epsilon},Y_{\epsilon^{\prime}}}((\epsilon-\epsilon^{\prime}){\sf a})},

where

(B.25)

\displaystyle N_{\lambda,\mu}(x)=\prod_{s\in\lambda}\left(x+a_{\lambda}(s)+l_{\mu}(s)+1\right)\prod_{t\in\mu}\left(x-a_{\mu}(t)-l_{\lambda}(t)-1\right),

with
a_{Y}(\Box)
and
l_{Y}(\Box)
denoting the arm and leg length of the box
\Box
in the Young diagram
Y
respectively.
In the limit
\tau\rightarrow\mathbf{i}\infty
, the RHS of (
B.21
) reads

(B.26)

\displaystyle\frac{Z_{0}^{D}(\tau)}{Z_{1/2}^{D}(\tau)}

\displaystyle=q^{-1/2}\left[\frac{C_{0}}{C_{-1/2}e^{-\mathbf{i}\nu/2}q^{-2{\sf a}}+C_{1/2}e^{\mathbf{i}\nu/2}q^{2\sf a}}+\mathcal{O}(q)\right].

The coefficients
C_{0},C_{\pm 1/2}
can be simplified using the relation
G(z+1)=\Gamma(z)G(z)
:

(B.27)

\displaystyle C_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{G(1-m+2{\sf a})G(1-m-2{\sf a})}{G(1+2{\sf a})G(1-2{\sf a})}\times\frac{G(1+2{\sf a})G(1-2{\sf a})}{G(1-m+2{\sf a})G(1-m-2{\sf a})}=1,

\displaystyle C_{-1/2}

\displaystyle=\frac{G(1-m+2({\sf a}-1/2))G(1-m-2({\sf a}-1/2))}{G(1+2({\sf a}-1/2))G(1-2({\sf a}-1/2))}\times\frac{G(1+2{\sf a})G(1-2{\sf a})}{G(1-m+2{\sf a})G(1-m-2{\sf a})}

(B.28)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(2{\sf a})\Gamma(1-m-2{\sf a})}{\Gamma(2{\sf a}-m)\Gamma(1-2{\sf a})},

\displaystyle C_{1/2}

\displaystyle=\frac{G(1-m+2({\sf a}+1/2))G(1-m-2({\sf a}+1/2))}{G(1+2({\sf a}+1/2))G(1-2({\sf a}+1/2))}\times\frac{G(1+2{\sf a})G(1-2{\sf a})}{G(1-m+2{\sf a})G(1-m-2{\sf a})}

(B.29)

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{m^{2}}{4{\sf a}^{2}}\right)\frac{\Gamma(2{\sf a}-m)\Gamma(1-2{\sf a})}{\Gamma(1-m-2{\sf a})\Gamma(2{\sf a})}.

Substituting (
B.28
), (
B.29
),(
B.20
) in (
B.26
) we get that

\displaystyle\frac{Z_{0}^{D}(\tau)}{Z_{1/2}^{D}(\tau)}

\displaystyle=q^{-1/2}\left[\frac{1}{e^{-2\pi\mathbf{i}\beta}q^{-2{\sf a}}+e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}\beta}q^{2{\sf a}}}+\mathcal{O}(q)\right]

(B.30)

\displaystyle=\frac{q^{-1/2}}{2\cos(2\pi({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b}))}(1+\mathcal{O}(\cos(4\pi({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b}))).

Now for the LHS of (
B.21
),
substituting the series expansions of the theta functions (
A.4
), (
A.5
), we see that

(B.31)

\displaystyle\frac{\theta_{3}(2u|2\tau)}{\theta_{2}(2u|2\tau)}

\displaystyle=\frac{q^{-1/2}}{2\cos(2\pi u)}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(q^{2})\right).

Comparing the RHS (
B.30
) and the LHS (
B.31
),

\displaystyle 2\cos(2u)

\displaystyle=e^{-2\pi i{\sf b}}q^{-2{\sf a}}+e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}\beta}q^{2{\sf a}}-\frac{m^{2}}{4{\sf a}^{2}}e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}{\sf b}}q^{2{\sf a}}

(B.32)

\displaystyle=2\cos(2\pi({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b}))-\frac{m^{2}}{4{\sf a}^{2}}e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b})}.

We can therefore make the following ansatz for
u(\tau)
in the limit
\tau\rightarrow\mathbf{i}\infty
:

(B.33)

u(\tau)={\sf a}\tau+{\sf b}+\frac{1}{2\pi\mathbf{i}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k=-n}^{\infty}c_{n,k}q^{n}e^{4\pi\mathbf{i}k({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b})}={\sf a}\tau+{\sf b}+\frac{X}{2\pi\mathbf{i}}.

The first derivative

(B.34)

\frac{u(\tau)}{d\tau}={\sf a}+\frac{1}{2\pi\mathbf{i}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k=-n}^{\infty}c_{n,k}(2\pi\mathbf{i})\left(n+2k\right)q^{n}e^{4\pi\mathbf{i}k({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b})},

and the second derivative

(B.35)

\frac{d^{2}u(\tau)}{d\tau^{2}}=(2\pi\mathbf{i})\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k=-n}^{\infty}c_{n,k}(n+2k{\sf a})^{2}q^{n}e^{4\pi\mathbf{i}k({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b})}.

Let us now look at our equation

(B.36)

\displaystyle(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}\frac{d^{2}u(\tau)}{d\tau^{2}}=m^{2}\wp^{\prime}(2u|\tau).

The Weierstrass
\wp
function has the following expression in terms of theta functions

(B.37)

\displaystyle\wp(z|\tau)=-\partial_{z}^{2}\log\theta_{1}(z|\tau)-2\eta_{1}(\tau),

and we now analyze the term
\partial_{z}^{2}\log\theta_{1}(z|\tau)
in the limit
q\rightarrow 0
. Starting from the series representation of the theta function

(B.38)

\displaystyle\theta_{1}(z|\tau)=-\mathbf{i}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{n}q^{(n+\frac{1}{2})^{2}}e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}z(n+\frac{1}{2})},

the logarithmic derivative

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\log\theta_{1}(z|\tau)-\pi\cot\pi z

\displaystyle=4\pi\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q^{k}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}q^{kn}\sin(2\pi kz)

(B.39)

\displaystyle=-(2\pi\mathbf{i})\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}q^{kn}e^{\mathbf{i}2\pi kz}-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}q^{kn}e^{-\mathbf{i}2\pi kz}\right).

The above computation implies that

(B.40)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{2}\log\theta_{1}(z|\tau)+\pi^{2}\csc^{2}\pi z

\displaystyle=-(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}kq^{kn}e^{\mathbf{i}2\pi kz}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}kq^{kn}e^{-\mathbf{i}2\pi kz}\right).

Moreover,
\csc^{2}(z)
has the following series representation:

(B.41)

\displaystyle\pi^{2}\csc^{2}\pi z=\frac{\pi^{2}}{\sin^{2}\pi z}

\displaystyle=(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}e^{2\mathbf{i}\pi z}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}ke^{2\mathbf{i}\pi(k-1)z}=(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}ke^{2\mathbf{i}\pi kz}.

Combining (
B.41
) and (
B.40
) we get

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{2}\log\theta_{1}(z|\tau)

\displaystyle=-(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}kq^{kn}e^{\mathbf{i}2\pi kz}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}kq^{kn}e^{-\mathbf{i}2\pi kz}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}ke^{2\mathbf{i}\pi kz}\right)

(B.42)

\displaystyle=-(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}kq^{kn}e^{\mathbf{i}2\pi kz}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}kq^{kn}e^{-\mathbf{i}2\pi kz}\right).

Therefore,

(B.43)

\displaystyle\wp^{\prime}(z|\tau)=-\partial_{z}^{3}\log\theta_{1}(z|\tau)=(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{3}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}k^{2}q^{kn}e^{\mathbf{i}2\pi kz}-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}k^{2}q^{kn}e^{-\mathbf{i}2\pi kz}\right).

Substituting (
B.35
), (
B.43
) in (
B.36
) we get

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k=-n}^{\infty}c_{n,k}(n+2k{\sf a})^{2}q^{n}e^{4\pi\mathbf{i}k({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b})}

\displaystyle=m^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}k^{2}q^{kn}e^{4\pi\mathbf{i}k({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b})}e^{kX}

(B.44)

\displaystyle-m^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}k^{2}q^{kn}e^{-4\pi\mathbf{i}k({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b})}e^{-kX}.

Computing the first few values of
c_{n,k}
:

(B.45)

\displaystyle c_{0,0}

\displaystyle=0,

(B.46)

\displaystyle c_{0,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{m^{2}}{4{\sf a}^{2}},

(B.47)

\displaystyle c_{1,-1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{m^{2}}{(1-2{\sf a})^{2}}

Therefore,

(B.48)

\displaystyle u(\tau)={\sf a}\tau+{\sf b}+\frac{m^{2}}{8\pi\mathbf{i}{\sf a}^{2}}e^{4\pi\mathbf{i}({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b})}+\mathcal{O}(qe^{4\pi\mathbf{i}({\sf a}\tau+{\sf b})}).

∎

Proposition B.2
.

Near its zero
u(\tau_{\star})=0
, the solution of the equation (
B.2
)
u(\tau)
behaves as

(B.49)

\displaystyle u(\tau)=e^{\mp\mathbf{i}\pi/4}\sqrt{\frac{m}{2\pi}}(\tau-\tau_{\star})^{1/2}\left(1\pm\frac{H_{\star}}{4\pi\mathbf{i}m}(\tau-\tau_{\star})\right)+\mathcal{O}\left((\tau-\tau_{\star})^{5/2}\right).

Proof.

The equation (
B.2
) near
u(\tau)=0
reads as

(B.50)

\displaystyle(2\pi i)^{2}\frac{d^{2}u(\tau)}{d\tau^{2}}=-\frac{m^{2}}{4u(\tau)^{3}}

due to the following behaviour of the Weierstrass
\wp
function at zero

(B.51)

\displaystyle\lim_{z\rightarrow 0}\wp(z)=\frac{1}{z^{2}}.

The solution
u(\tau)
therefore has the following behaviour

(B.52)

\displaystyle u(\tau)=c_{1}\sqrt{\tau-\tau_{\star}}+c_{2}(\tau-\tau_{\star})^{3/2}+\mathcal{O}((\tau-\tau_{\star})^{5/2})=c_{1}(\tau-\tau_{\star})^{1/2}\left(1+\frac{c_{2}}{c_{1}}(\tau-\tau_{\star})\right)+\mathcal{O}((\tau-\tau_{\star})^{5/2}),

and the second derivative of the above function

(B.53)

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}u(\tau)}{d\tau^{2}}=-\frac{c_{1}}{4}(\tau-\tau_{\star})^{-3/2}\left(1-\frac{3c_{2}}{c_{1}}(\tau-\tau_{\star})\right).

Substituting (
B.52
), (
B.53
) in (
B.50
), we obtain that

(B.54)

\displaystyle c_{1}=e^{\mp\mathbf{i}\pi/4}\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}.

The coefficient of the sub-leading term
c_{2}
can be obtained by studying the Hamiltonian in the same limit:

(B.55)

\displaystyle H_{\star}

\displaystyle:=\lim_{\tau\to\tau_{\star}}H

\displaystyle=\lim_{\tau\to\tau_{\star}}\left((2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}\left(\frac{du(\tau)}{d\tau}\right)^{2}-\frac{m^{2}}{4u(\tau)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathop{=}^{\eqref{ansatz}}(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{4}(\tau-\tau_{\star})^{-1}\left(1+\frac{6c_{2}}{c_{1}}(\tau-\tau_{\star})\right)-m^{2}\frac{1}{4c_{1}^{2}}(\tau-\tau_{\star})^{-1}\left(1-2\frac{c_{2}}{c_{1}}(\tau-\tau_{\star})\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau-\tau_{\star})

\displaystyle\mathop{=}^{\eqref{c1}}\frac{c_{2}}{2c_{1}}\left(3(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}\frac{m}{(2\pi)}e^{\mp\mathbf{i}\pi/2}+\frac{m^{2}(2\pi)}{m}e^{\pm\mathbf{i}\pi/2}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau-\tau_{\star})

\displaystyle=\pm\frac{c_{2}}{c_{1}}\left(4\pi\mathbf{i}m\right)+\mathcal{O}(\tau-\tau_{\star}).

(B.56)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\frac{c_{2}}{c_{1}}

\displaystyle=\pm\frac{H_{\star}}{4\pi\mathbf{i}m}.

With the constants
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
above,

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\rightarrow\tau_{\star}}u(\tau)=e^{\mp\mathbf{i}\pi/4}\sqrt{\frac{m}{2\pi}}(\tau-\tau_{\star})^{1/2}\left(1\pm\frac{H_{\star}}{4\pi\mathbf{i}m}(\tau-\tau_{\star})\right)+\mathcal{O}\left((\tau-\tau_{\star})^{5/2}\right).

∎
